<pasted_content id="4bb7">
# Nexus v1.1.2: Non-Progress Recovery and Execution-State Continuity

You are operating inside the Nexus Code repository as a senior principal engineer responsible for agent orchestration, execution reliability, recovery mechanisms, and regression prevention.

Your assignment is to investigate, design, implement, test, and independently validate improvements to Nexus's non-progress detection and recovery behavior.

**This is an implementation assignment, not a documentation-only exercise.**

## 1. Incident Context

During a live Nexus dogfooding session, the engineering agent repeatedly inspected repository files despite having already acquired the relevant information.

Observed behavior:

- The agent inspected `cli/src/doctor.ts` and `cli/test/doctor.test.ts`.
- The agent explicitly recognized that it needed to stop re-reading files.
- Subsequent reasoning repeatedly regenerated nearly identical plans.
- Equivalent file reads continued without meaningful progress.
- Nexus detected repeated `read_file` observations with unchanged results.
- Two recovery instructions failed to produce a materially different approach.
- The workspace remained unchanged.
- Nexus eventually stopped execution with `too many steps without progress`.

Representative evidence:

```text
The agent repeated read_file with an unchanged result:
Nexus told it to change approach (2 of 2).

Stopped at too many steps without progress

repeated read_file 3 times with an unchanged result
on an unchanged workspace after 2 recovery notes
```

Initial assessment:

- Non-progress detection: functioning for this scenario.
- Bounded termination: functioning.
- Recovery instruction effectiveness: insufficient.
- Execution-state continuity: potentially defective.
- Semantic equivalence detection: potentially incomplete.
- Recovery escalation: insufficiently enforced.

These are hypotheses, not established root causes. Verify them against the implementation and available session evidence.

## 2. Primary Objective

Improve Nexus so that detecting non-progress leads to a constrained, evidence-driven change in execution strategy rather than repeated advisory instructions.

Nexus must distinguish among:

1. New information acquired.
2. A meaningful change in the agent's working hypothesis.
3. A productive execution action.
4. Repeated observation of existing information.
5. Repeated planning without state advancement.
6. Legitimate, intentional reinspection following a material state change.

**Success means improved task completion and recovery effectiveness, not merely fewer tool calls.**

Do not weaken existing loop detection or termination safeguards to make the agent appear more successful.

## 3. Repository and Baseline Discovery

Before implementation:

1. Inspect the current repository state and branch.
2. Identify existing uncommitted, staged, and untracked changes.
3. Identify the current v1.1.2 roadmap and Continuous Evaluation and Regression Intelligence workstream.
4. Locate the orchestration loop, tool execution pipeline, progress detection, recovery instructions, context management, session persistence, and termination logic.
5. Identify existing tests and regression fixtures covering non-progress, duplicate observations, and recovery.

Preserve all pre-existing changes.

Do not reset, discard, overwrite, or commit unrelated work.

Avoid repeatedly reading entire large files. Prefer targeted searches, symbol-level inspection, and bounded file ranges.

Record the baseline before modifying behavior.

## 4. Required Engineering Work

### 4.1 Semantic Observation Identity

Investigate how Nexus determines that a tool observation has already been seen.

Design and implement semantic equivalence detection sufficient to recognize materially redundant observations across tool invocation variants.

Examples:

```text
read_file cli/src/doctor.ts
cat cli/src/doctor.ts
sed -n '1,500p' cli/src/doctor.ts
head -500 cli/src/doctor.ts
```

These may provide equivalent observations even though the tool names and arguments differ.

Requirements:

- Normalize resource identities and file paths.
- Account for inspected content ranges.
- Track content fingerprints where practical.
- Distinguish exact duplication from substantial overlap.
- Account for workspace revisions and content changes.
- Avoid treating legitimate rereads after file modifications as duplicates.
- Avoid broad semantic similarity rules that incorrectly classify different evidence as identical.
- Keep fingerprint and observation metadata bounded.
- Do not retain redundant raw file contents unnecessarily.

Investigate existing infrastructure before introducing new abstractions.

### 4.2 Structured Recovery Escalation

Replace repeated advisory-only recovery with a bounded escalation mechanism.

Target conceptual states:

```text
NORMAL
  |
  v
NON_PROGRESS_DETECTED
  |
  v
RECOVERY_ADVISORY
  |
  v
RECOVERY_CONSTRAINED
  |
  +--> PRODUCTIVE_PROGRESS --> NORMAL
  |
  +--> NO_VIABLE_ALTERNATIVE --> TERMINATED
  |
  +--> RECOVERY_EXHAUSTED --> TERMINATED
```

Adapt state names and implementation to the existing architecture.

Requirements:

- First occurrence may produce an advisory.
- Repeated equivalent observations must trigger enforceable constraints.
- Nexus must reject equivalent unproductive observations during constrained recovery.
- Tool denial must be deterministic and explainable.
- Denied tool calls must not execute.
- Denial alone must not count as productive progress.
- Denied calls must not reset stagnation counters.
- Recovery must remain bounded.
- Existing step, token, time, and tool budgets must remain enforceable.
- Do not introduce an unbounded loop of rejected actions.

### 4.3 Recovery Context Contract

When entering constrained recovery, provide the agent with compact, structured context containing:

```text
RECOVERY_REQUIRED

Current objective:
[Known task objective]

Previously acquired evidence:
[Relevant observations already available]

Repeated observations:
[Resources, ranges, and fingerprints]

Current workspace state:
[Known revision or change marker]

Last meaningful progress:
[Evidence-backed progress event]

Unresolved question:
[Specific remaining information requirement, if known]

Disallowed operations:
[Equivalent observations already proven unproductive]

Permitted next actions:
[Alternative evidence acquisition or execution paths]

Recovery budget:
[Remaining attempts and applicable limits]
```

Do not fabricate an unresolved question or infer progress from the model's own claims.

If the unresolved question is not known, require the agent to identify it.

Reuse existing session context and evidence mechanisms where possible.

### 4.4 Execution-State Continuity

Investigate whether the agent is repeatedly reconstructing the same plan because relevant execution state is lost, compacted, omitted, or not incorporated into subsequent turns.

Implement the minimum necessary improvements to preserve:

- Current objective.
- Completed investigative steps.
- Evidence already acquired.
- Open questions.
- Active execution hypothesis.
- Last meaningful progress.
- Recovery state.
- Explicitly disallowed repetitive operations.

Do not create an entirely new planning subsystem unless existing architecture demonstrably cannot support the requirement.

Context compaction and session resume must preserve recovery-critical state.

The agent must not be able to escape recovery constraints merely by triggering compaction, resuming a session, or switching between equivalent tool interfaces.

### 4.5 Progress Classification

Introduce or improve progress classification so Nexus does not equate tool execution with useful work.

At minimum, distinguish:

```text
OBSERVATION_NEW
OBSERVATION_DUPLICATE
OBSERVATION_OVERLAPPING
HYPOTHESIS_UPDATED
ARTIFACT_MODIFIED
VALIDATION_EXECUTED
VALIDATION_PASSED
VALIDATION_FAILED
RECOVERY_ACTION
RECOVERY_DENIED
NO_PROGRESS
```

These are conceptual categories. Reuse established event types where appropriate.

Important constraints:

- A model statement claiming new understanding is not independently sufficient evidence of progress.
- A changed command string is not necessarily a new observation.
- A failed validation may constitute useful progress when it produces new diagnostic evidence.
- A file modification is not automatically productive if it is unrelated or repeatedly reverted.
- Legitimate read-only tasks must be able to complete without workspace changes.
- Repeated reads may be legitimate when the underlying artifact or relevant assumptions change.

Do not build an arbitrary scoring system unless deterministic rules cannot meet the requirements.

### 4.6 Recovery-Aware Tool Enforcement

Place enforcement at the appropriate execution boundary.

Constraints must apply before the tool action executes, regardless of how the model phrases the intended operation.

Investigate bypass paths involving:

- Native file-reading tools.
- Shell commands.
- Equivalent command substitutions.
- Tool aliases.
- Batched tool calls.
- Session resume.
- Context compaction.
- Worker/model changes.

**Do not claim universal semantic equivalence across arbitrary shell commands unless it is genuinely implemented and independently tested.**

For operations that cannot be reliably classified before execution, explicitly document the enforcement boundary and implement conservative handling that preserves legitimate work.

Do not silently claim complete mediation where only partial interception exists.

## 5. Continuous Evaluation and Regression Intelligence

Integrate this failure into the existing v1.1.2 evaluation workstream.

Create a deterministic regression fixture based on the observed scenario.

The fixture must cover:

1. Initial inspection of repository files.
2. Repeated equivalent observations.
3. Non-progress detection.
4. First recovery advisory.
5. Continued equivalent observations.
6. Escalation to constrained recovery.
7. Tool denial where supported.
8. Agent selection of an alternative operation.
9. Productive continuation or bounded termination.
10. Preservation of recovery state across compaction and resume.

Include adversarial variants:

- Identical read through another tool.
- Overlapping but nonidentical line ranges.
- Repeated full-file reads.
- Relevant file modified between reads.
- Shell command aliases.
- Model repeatedly acknowledging instructions without changing behavior.
- Agent generating slightly different plans without acquiring new evidence.
- Read-only investigation where no file modification is expected.
- Legitimate test rerun after changing code.
- Recovery budget exhaustion.
- Tool denial followed by another equivalent attempted operation.
- Session resume while constrained recovery is active.

Assert outcomes using execution evidence, not only model-generated text.

## 6. Acceptance Criteria

The implementation is not complete until the following are demonstrated.

### Functional correctness

- Duplicate observations are detected within the supported enforcement scope.
- Repeated equivalent observations cause constrained recovery.
- Enforced denials prevent supported redundant operations from executing.
- Recovery state survives relevant context and session lifecycle transitions.
- Meaningful alternative operations remain available.
- Legitimate rereads after material changes are permitted.
- Read-only tasks are not incorrectly blocked.

### Reliability

- No unbounded recovery loops.
- No infinite retry-after-denial cycles.
- Existing termination safeguards remain functional.
- Existing budget ceilings remain enforceable.
- Recovery failure produces a clear terminal reason.
- Failure handling does not corrupt session state.

### Observability

Emit structured, correlated evidence for:

```text
non_progress_detected
recovery_advisory_issued
recovery_constrained
observation_equivalent
tool_execution_denied
recovery_progress_detected
recovery_exhausted
recovery_completed
```

Map these to existing event schemas where possible.

Evidence must identify:

- Session and relevant step.
- Triggering observation/action.
- Reason for the classification.
- Recovery state transition.
- Enforced restriction.
- Outcome.

Avoid leaking sensitive file contents or credentials into telemetry.

### Regression safety

- Existing tests pass.
- New tests exercise actual enforcement paths.
- Existing normal execution is not materially degraded.
- Session replay remains valid.
- No unrelated interfaces are broken.

## 7. Independent Validation

Do not treat newly written tests passing as independent confirmation.

Perform separate validation using the strongest available mechanism:

1. Existing integration harness.
2. Session replay against a controlled fixture.
3. Actual Nexus agent execution against a disposable repository.
4. Existing independent reviewer or evaluator, if available.

Where feasible, compare baseline and candidate behavior using identical tasks and controlled model settings.

Capture:

- Task completion result.
- Duplicate observation attempts.
- Duplicate observations actually executed.
- Recovery transitions.
- Denied tool executions.
- Time and token consumption.
- Termination reason.
- False-positive restrictions.
- Evidence of successful continuation.

Do not claim improved task-completion rates from one successful execution.

If model behavior is stochastic, distinguish deterministic enforcement validation from empirical recovery effectiveness.

Do not allow an internally generated evaluation score to serve as the sole proof of effectiveness.

## 8. Implementation Discipline

Follow existing Nexus engineering conventions.

Required operating rules:

- No destructive repository cleanup.
- No unrelated refactoring.
- No weakening current safety or budget controls.
- No new dependencies without justification.
- No broad exceptions that suppress non-progress detection.
- No simulated results represented as live results.
- No acceptance based solely on self-assessment.
- No scope expansion into cosmetic UI changes.
- No automatic production deployment.

Preserve current user work and maintain compatibility with supported model endpoints.

Prioritize deterministic execution and evidence over model-specific prompt tricks.

## 9. Required Deliverables

Produce:

1. Root-cause assessment with supporting code and session evidence.
2. Architecture decision explaining the selected enforcement boundaries.
3. Implemented recovery improvements.
4. Deterministic unit and integration tests.
5. Regression fixture integrated with v1.1.2 Continuous Evaluation and Regression Intelligence.
6. Evidence from independent validation.
7. Updated roadmap and implementation status.
8. Remaining limitations and bypass scenarios.
9. Final acceptance report.

Final report format:

```text
NEXUS v1.1.2 NON-PROGRESS RECOVERY

Baseline:
Root cause:
Implementation:
Enforcement boundary:
Tests:
Independent validation:
Regression results:
Known limitations:
Remaining risks:
Files changed:
Commit(s):
Acceptance status:
```

Use explicit acceptance states:

```text
PASS
PARTIAL
FAIL
NOT_TESTED
BLOCKED
```

A passing implementation requires evidence that Nexus both detects and enforces recovery constraints without introducing unacceptable false positives or bypasses.

## 10. Execution Authorization

Proceed with repository discovery, implementation planning, code modifications, test development, and local validation.

Do not stop after producing a plan.

Implement the smallest coherent production-quality solution that meets the verified requirements.

You may commit verified, in-scope changes using the repository's established workflow, but do not commit unrelated existing changes.

Do not deploy to production or modify live infrastructure.

If full semantic command interception, deterministic replay, or independent evaluation is not currently supported, implement the defensible subset, expose the limitation explicitly, and identify the remaining work.

**Begin by inspecting the existing progress and recovery implementation, then execute the work through validation and reporting.**

review PR #398 independently

<pasted_content id="4bb7">
Findings
Medium
Code review
·
7m ago
·
batch_read returns overall success with per-file errors (core/src/file_ops.rs:1010-1018,1186-1189), but dropping failed entries lets the successful subset represent the entire observation. After reading a.ts, successive batches containing a.ts plus a different missing path are classified as duplicates (cli/src/core/execution_tracking.ts:498-511) and eventually exhaust recovery despite discovering new errors. Account for those outcomes before treating the whole batch as equivalent.

Code review
·
7m ago
·
An ignored file such as build.log can change without changing this state: candidate enumeration excludes ignored files (cli/src/core/candidate.ts:565-567), and the supplemental identity covers only fixed markers (cli/src/agent.ts:759-767,2682-2687). Once constrained, cached coverage and EOF therefore cause new log output to be refused without executing the read, eventually stopping legitimate monitoring. Verify the target file's identity before refusing, or allow reads of files outside the workspace identity's coverage.

Code review
·
7m ago
·
Presence is keyed only by tool and arguments (cli/src/agent.ts:380-387,438-451): reading a file on S1, compacting that output away, reading the same arguments on S2, then restoring S1 makes S1's coverage appear present because S2's output shares its key. During constrained recovery, this refuses reacquisition even though the conversation contains the wrong version, and repeated attempts terminate the turn. Track presence by execution identity or by state and output identity.

Code review
·
7m ago
·
When the first call in a sequential batch escalates recovery to constrained, the next three covered reads can immediately exhaust the default refusal budget, although the constraining note is only delivered in endStep (cli/src/agent.ts:5616-5641). This stops the turn and skips later work in that batch before the model can respond to the note. Activate refusal on the following step, or exempt the constraining batch from the denial budget.

Slop
·
8m ago
·
While constrained, the full RECOVERY_REQUIRED block is pushed into history as a user message (the constrained note, via this.recoveryContext). renderPinnedLedger also renders it again as a pinned system block on every request. The note's 'refusals left' and evidence-presence lines are frozen at the moment the note was written, while the pinned copy is current. The model therefore sees two RECOVERY_REQUIRED blocks that contradict each other on the budget. Keep the context in one place, e.g. make the note point to the pinned block.

Slop
·
8m ago
·
ProgressClass (7 values) and the verdict's classification and equivalent fields are computed on every observe(), but no production code reads them. agent.ts only uses equivalentLoop/equivalence, and only the unit tests assert classification. This is speculative API surface that has to be maintained alongside informative/repeat, which already drive progress. Remove it, or wire it into the progress evaluation that is meant to use it.

Low
Slop
·
8m ago
·
In the parallel read path, every call is judged first, and refuse() can set stopReason partway through the batch. Calls judged before that point are still dispatched afterward via executed. So a turn that recovery has already stopped still runs reads, even though the sequential path's comment says "nothing more runs". Drop those calls (with a not-executed message) once stopReason is set.

Slop
·
8m ago
·
assert.ok(repeatRuns.length <= constrainedAtRequest, "no repeat executed after the constrained note") compares a count of executions against a request index. Because the repeated pair runs at most once per request, this is nearly always true and does not check that no repeat ran after the constrained note. Filter the dispatch log to calls issued after that request and assert the result is empty.

Slop
·
8m ago
·
The compaction continuity test branches on what actually happened (if (readOutputPresent) … else …) and then asserts the disjunction denials >= 1 || stage === "normal". Whichever way compaction goes, the test accepts it, so it doesn't pin down the claimed behavior that a read is refused only while its output is still in the conversation. Set up a fixture where the output is deterministically kept (or removed) and assert that single outcome.

</pasted_content id="4bb7">

<result>**Verdict: REQUEST CHANGES** for PR #398 at `ff2e2ef1`.

All nine of the earlier findings are fixed or partly fixed, and all nine suites you listed pass. But I reproduced a crash when a session saved by an older build is restored, and a refusal-and-stop on a legitimate read-only pattern that the base build completes. The review worktree at `/home/mike/nexus-wt/pr398-review` is clean at `ff2e2ef1` and left in place. The probe scripts are in `/tmp/claude-1000/-home-mike-nexus-code/ee800dc3-6bb6-4e55-89e6-d6c2e85af4b6/scratchpad/` (`probe.test.mts`, `upgrade.mts`, `oldledger.mts`, `shell.mts`). Builds were clean: `cargo build --locked --release`, then `npm ci &amp;&amp; npm run build` (tsc).

## Findings, by severity

**1. High: crash after restoring a session saved before v1.1.2** (`cli/src/core/objective.ts:594-595`; reproduced)
- `renderPinnedLedger` reads `tracker.recoveryStage` straight from `ledger.execution`, which is never normalized. A ledger written by an older build has no `execution.recovery` field.
- Restoring a session doesn't normalize the ledger either (`agent.ts:1655`). The only call that does, `trackingOf`, runs at the start of `runTurn`.
- So whenever the ledger has to be pinned (a verifier attempt, gating requirements, or the objective compacted out), anything that builds request messages before the first turn throws. That includes `/compact` (repl, slash command, sidecar `compactNow`) and `estimateTurnAdmission`, which the operation runner calls.
- How I reproduced it: I ran a turn with a verifier on base `9a62db99` and saved the session, then restored it on head:
  ```
  restored; execution has recovery: false
  gating 0 verification 1 objInContext true
  estimateTurnAdmission THREW TypeError Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'stage')
  compactConversation THREW TypeError Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'stage')
  runTurn(resume) OK VERIFICATION_NOT_RUN
  ```
- At unit level the same old ledger renders fine on base (`rendered OK, length 136`) and throws on head.
- `ledgerNeedsPinning` already uses `recovery?.stage`. The fix is to normalize the tracking (or use `?.`) in `renderPinnedLedger`, and to normalize on restore.

**2. High: legitimate read-only work gets refused and the turn stops** (`execution_tracking.ts:502-507`, `agent.ts:5610`, note text at `agent.ts:5629`; reproduced, regression against base)
- The equivalence counter is per file and state, and the loop threshold is 2. So one full read followed by two narrower reads of *different* line ranges counts as a loop.
- Probe P1: read the whole 300-line file once, then six distinct 20-line ranges, with no edits.
  - Head: `STAGNATION_DETECTED recovery exhausted after 2 recovery notes: 3 equivalent observation(s) refused`, ran 4 of 7.
  - Base: completes, all 7 ran.
- The note tells the model it "received the same result each time", which is false when the ranges differ.
- The existing test "a read-only investigation of distinct files" only does one narrow re-read, so it doesn't cover this. The doc's limitations section doesn't mention it either.

**3. Medium: other workspace-observing tools ignore the file-signature fix** (`execution_tracking.ts:664-667`; reproduced)
- Earlier issue 2 is fixed for `read_file`, `batch_read` and the shell reads. But the exact-repeat refusal for the other workspace-observing tools still trusts only the workspace state identity. Those tools are `file_info`, `list_dir`, `search_code`, `lsp_diagnostics`, `semantic_search`, and `read_file` with `offset`/`length`.
- Probe P5: a background writer appends to an ignored `build.log`. Run `file_info build.log`, constrain with repeated `f.txt` reads, then `file_info build.log` again. Head returns `[Nexus refused: not executed] file_info {"path":"build.log"}: the same call already ran on this unchanged workspace (step 1)…` while the file grew (now 141 bytes). Base runs it.
- Results from the LSP and semantic-index tools also depend on server or index state, not only on the workspace.

**4. Low: one parallel batch can use up the whole refusal budget** (`agent.ts:5990`, `execution_tracking.ts:675`; reproduced)
- Refusals are counted per call, while escalation is limited to once per step.
- Probe P3: after the constraining note, a batch of `[g.txt (new), f.txt×3, g.txt 2-3]` gave 3 refusals and a stop, so the new `g.txt` read never ran.
- Result order matched `tool_calls` (`P3 order ok true`). Base also stops on this script, so it isn't a regression in outcome.

**5. Low / nits** (code reading only)
- `verdict.classification` (`execution_tracking.ts:512-517`) is still computed for every call but nothing in `src` reads it. Only the internal "duplicate means not informative" use matters.
- `presence()` scans every message on every tool call, even when recovery is in its normal stage, because it is evaluated eagerly at `agent.ts:5643` and inside `observe`.
- The file signature is taken after the read, so a write by another process between the read and the stat could pair old coverage with a new signature. This is a narrow race.
- The doc says the `cd DIR &amp;&amp;` prefix must resolve to the workspace root, but the code accepts any subdirectory (`cd src &amp;&amp; cat a.ts` gives `src/a.ts`). The behaviour is fine; the doc is wrong.

## The nine earlier findings

| # | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| 1 batch_read with per-file errors | Fixed | `observation_identity.ts:191-193` attributes nothing if any file failed or is missing; there is a unit test at line 61. |
| 2 ignored files | Partly fixed | Reads check the file signature (`judgeCoverage`, `recoveryDenial:651-653`). Other observing tools don't (finding 3). |
| 3 presence keyed by tool+args | Fixed | Coverage is keyed by call id and filtered by state and signature; there is a unit test for it. |
| 4 budget used up in the constraining step | Fixed | `recoveryDenial` returns early when `lastEscalationStep === step`; `recordUnproductive` treats repeats in the same step as one. |
| 5 RECOVERY_REQUIRED duplicated | Fixed for durable sessions | The note points to the pinned copy, and the incident test asserts the note has no `Recovery budget:`. Non-durable sessions still get one frozen copy, by design. |
| 6 unused fields | Partly fixed | `equivalent` was removed; `classification` is still unused (finding 5). |
| 7 parallel calls judged before a stopping refusal still dispatched | Fixed | P3: `g.txt`, judged before the stop, did not run. |
| 8 vacuous incident assertion | Fixed | It now asserts no repeat ran with `afterRequests &gt; constrainedAtRequest`, together with at least 2 refusals and at least one earlier run. |
| 9 test accepted either outcome | Fixed | Split into two deterministic tests: output kept means refused; output cut means one re-acquisition then a refusal. The cut is simulated by rewriting the message with Nexus's truncation marker. |

## Other checks (no defects found)
- **Bounds:** `maxRecoveries` 0 still stops on the first loop, the stagnation window is unchanged, and refusals produce no verdict, so they never count as progress (the ledger identity covers requirements only). Backoff is untouched.
- **Persistence:** `normalizeTracking` fills in `recovery`, `coverage`, `equivalents` and `evicted.coverage`. Growth is capped (512 coverage segments, 128 equivalence records, 64 events) and only 16-hex digests are stored, never file content.
- **Shell parsing** (`shell.mts`): no trailing newline, CRLF, empty files, `sed` past EOF (no coverage), `head -n 10` on a 3-line file (total 3), `cd ..` (rejected), pipes and `;` (rejected) all behave correctly. `cat -` maps to a path `-`, which is harmless.
- **Folded or truncated output:** run_command output is one JSON line, so diagnostic hints and folding can't cut stdout while it still counts as delivered. My P2 attempt confirmed this: the refusal there was correct.

Both High findings from my last review are fixed and re-probed, and so is the Medium one. No unbounded loop and no escaped loop pattern turned up. All nine suites pass. ## Earlier findings, re-run on the new head

**1. Crash restoring a session saved before v1.1.2 (was High): fixed.**
- `objective.ts:594` now normalizes the tracking before building the tracker.
- I re-ran `upgrade.mts`: a fresh session with a verifier attempt saved by base `9a62db99`, then restored on head:
  ```
  restored; execution has recovery: false
  gating 0 verification 1 objInContext true
  requestMessages(after compaction drops objective) OK 3
  estimateTurnAdmission OK
  compactConversation OK Compacted context: distilled 2 messages, 5 active messages in memory. ...
  runTurn(resume) OK VERIFICATION_NOT_RUN
  ```
- `oldledger.mts` now prints `rendered OK, length 136`.
- Nit: the new normalize-on-restore at `agent.ts:1657` is on the task-recovery path only. `restoreSession` (`agent.ts:2003`, used by `restoreConversation`) still doesn't normalize; that is why the probe still prints `recovery: false`. The crash is covered by the normalization at render time, so this is not a defect.

**2. Full read followed by distinct slices refused and stopped (was High): fixed.**
- Equivalence now counts exact line ranges, not whole files.
- P1 output: `P1 reason undefined` (completed), `P1 ran 7 refused 0`, events were only `observation_equivalent` ×6, with no recovery episode.
- P4 output: `ran 6 refused 0`, completed.
- There is a new test covering this case.

**3. Exact repeat of `file_info` on a growing ignored file refused (was Medium): fixed.**
- `recoveryDenial` no longer refuses non-read tools at all.
- P5 output: both `file_info build.log` calls ran, `P5 refused []`, completed.

**4. One parallel batch uses up the whole refusal budget (was Low): not fixed, unchanged.**
- P3 output: `STAGNATION_DETECTED recovery exhausted ... 3 equivalent observation(s) refused`; the new `g.txt` read in the batch didn't run; `P3 order ok true`.
- Base also stops on this script, so it's not a regression.

**5. Nits: unchanged.**
- `classification` is still computed but never read in production.
- `presence()` still scans every message on every tool call.
- The signature-after-read race is still there.
- The `cd DIR &amp;&amp;` wording in the doc was revised; I didn't re-check it against the code.

## The two areas you asked about

**Can a repeat-only loop of a non-read tool now run unbounded? No.** These tools are no longer refused, but their exact repeats still escalate (note, then constrained), and the next run that executes ends the turn.

| Probe | Result |
|---|---|
| `file_info` repeated | `STAGNATION_DETECTED`, "repeated file_info 5 times ... after 2 recovery notes", ran 5 |
| `list_dir` repeated | same, ran 5 |
| `search_code` repeated | same, ran 5 |
| `read_file` with offset/length repeated | same, ran 5 |
| 4 identical `file_info` per step, in parallel | "repeated file_info 9 times ...", ran 12, 3 requests |

**Does exact-range equivalence let a loop escape detection? Nothing escapes, but some loops are caught later.**
- The incident fixture still passes.
- The commit rewrote the cross-interface test: `sed 1,20` became `sed 1,30` and `head -n 5` became `head -n 30`. The new script makes every call read the same range, so the test no longer covers loops that mix narrower ranges.
- N4, the original script on head:
  - Once through: only the advisory note fires, then it completes (`ran 7 refused 0`).
  - Repeated as a cycle: constrained, then `recovery exhausted ... 3 refused`, after 8 executions and 11 requests. At `ff2e2ef1` that was 4 executions and 7 requests.
- N3, the same range through five rotating forms (read_file, `sed`, `head`, `./`, `cd . &amp;&amp;`): exhausted after 4 executions, 3 refused.
- N3, alternating the incident's ranges 1-50 and 1-80: exhausted after 6 executions, 3 refused.
- N2, a sliding 10-line window over a file already in context: no recovery note at all. It stops on the stagnation window: "30 consecutive steps without progress", 31 requests.
- So mixed-range and sliding re-reads are bounded by the stagnation window rather than by refusal. The doc's lines 58-59 now say this.

Findings
Medium
Code review
·
1h ago
·
The parser preserves provider call IDs (cli/src/parser.ts:551-552), so different executions can share one. If compaction removes a read but retains an update_plan result with the same ID, this set marks the removed read as present; cli/src/core/execution_tracking.ts:653-659 then refuses its legitimate reacquisition and can exhaust recovery. Use a unique execution identifier shared by coverage and the stored result.

Code review
·
3h ago
·
With constrained recovery’s refusal budget spent, a sequential batch such as [read_file(new.ts), read_file(covered.ts), write_file(...)] observes new evidence but still stops on the second call, skipping the write. Recovery clears only at batch end (cli/src/agent.ts:5657-5668, cli/src/core/execution_tracking.ts:750-768), after this code has committed to STAGNATION_DETECTED. Apply the recorded progress before exhausting recovery on later calls.

Slop
·
3h ago
·
ProgressClass (7 values) and the verdict's classification and equivalent fields are computed on every observe(), but no production code reads them. agent.ts only uses equivalentLoop/equivalence, and only the unit tests assert classification. This is speculative API surface that has to be maintained alongside informative/repeat, which already drive progress. Remove it, or wire it into the progress evaluation that is meant to use it.

Low
Slop
·
3h ago
·
ObservationVerdict.equivalent was removed, but the doc comment on equivalence still says "The equivalence record behind equivalent". Point it at equivalentLoop or describe the record directly.

<result>**Verdict: REQUEST CHANGES** at `dcf2674c9d46d71cf51bcbc7c0326624cacebaaa`.

Findings 1, 3 and 4 are fixed. The fix for finding 2 also creates a regression that I reproduced: a model can repeat an identical write that changes nothing, and recovery never steps in.

## The four findings

**1. Call ids reused across executions: fixed.**
- `uniqueCallIds` (`cli/src/agent.ts:2642`) collects every id already in use: coverage keys, ids in assistant `tool_calls`, and ids in tool results. It renames any call that collides with one of them, or with an earlier call in the same batch.
- It runs before the push at `cli/src/agent.ts:5942`. The parser creates new call objects (`cli/src/parser.ts:552`, `cli/src/parser.ts:597`), so the raw provider message is not changed.
- Everything that uses `call.id` runs after the push and reads the same objects: dispatch, approval, `observe` (`callId`), refusals, and the tool-result `tool_call_id`. I found nothing that reads an id before the push. Token accounting stringifies the raw message, but it does not use the id.
- On resume or restore, the ids already in history go into the "in use" set.
- `stableToolCallId` hashes both sides of a pair the same way, so pairs still match on disk. Renamed short ids are about 17 characters, below its 20-character threshold.
- The new test passes. It checks that 6 calls all named `call_0` get unique ids, that results pair correctly, and that coverage keys belong only to the read calls.
- The scan of `this.messages` costs O(n) per step. That is no worse than `observationPresence(this.messages)`, which already runs once per call, so speed is not a concern.

**2. Batch stopped before the write: fixed for the reported batch.** The new test passes for `[new read, covered read, write]` with the refusal budget spent. See the regression below.

**3. Unused ProgressClass / classification: fixed.** `classification` is now read in production (`cli/src/agent.ts:5558`). `"ARTIFACT_MODIFIED"` is a real member of the union (`cli/src/core/execution_tracking.ts:395`). It is produced for any successful action in `MUTATORS` (`cli/src/core/execution_tracking.ts:513`). The `equivalent` field was already gone at 17addf0a.

**4. Stale doc comment: fixed** (`cli/src/core/execution_tracking.ts:419`).

## New finding (Medium): identical writes bypass recovery

**Location:** `cli/src/agent.ts:5558`, used at `cli/src/agent.ts:5644` and `cli/src/agent.ts:5674`.

**Cause:**
- `progressedThisStep()` counts any `ARTIFACT_MODIFIED` verdict as progress. That class is assigned to every successful write, including one that rewrites a file with the same content.
- The check also sees the verdict of the call currently being judged, because it is pushed before the check. So a write that is itself repeating unchanged (`verdict.loop` is true) returns before `recordUnproductive`.
- `evaluateStep` (`cli/src/core/execution_tracking.ts:738`) disagrees: it only counts a new workspace state, new information, a ledger change or operator input as progress. So the new comment "it ends the recovery episode at endStep" is false in this case. Recovery is suspended, but the episode never ends.

**Reproduction:** a throwaway probe test, run at both SHAs and then deleted. The gateway returns the same calls every step, with `stagnationSteps: 30` and `maxRecoveries: 2`.

| Scenario | 17addf0a | dcf2674c |
|---|---|---|
| `write_file(x.txt, "same\n", overwrite)` every step | stops at step 5; 2 notes, constrained then exhausted | 30 steps, 0 recovery notes, stopped only by stagnation |
| that write + `read_file(f.txt)` (already covered) every step | stops at step 5, 9 calls run | 31 steps, 62 calls run, 0 notes, 0 refusals |
| reads only (control) | stops at step 7, 3 refusals | stops at step 7, 3 refusals (unchanged) |

**Impact:**
- This is bounded, but only by `stagnationSteps` (default 12, so about 2.4 times the 17addf0a bound, which costs inference and tokens), and it removes the guarantee the recovery notes and refusals are meant to give.
- With a write whose content changes each time, `evaluateStep` does count progress. That was already true at 17addf0a, so it is not new.

**Suggested fix:**
- Count a write as progress only if the workspace actually reached a new state. For example: `v.informative || (v.classification === "ARTIFACT_MODIFIED" &amp;&amp; !v.repeat)`. Or compare `inputStateFor()` to the step's starting state and to the states already seen.
- Leave out the current call's own verdict when `observe` makes this check.
- Add a test with a write that changes nothing.

## Nit

The subagent loop (`cli/src/agent.ts:6758`) does not call `uniqueCallIds`. Nothing breaks, because the subagent tracker uses neither `callId` nor presence. But the docs say ids are unique per execution "in the conversation", and subagent histories can still hold duplicates.

<result>**Verdict: REQUEST CHANGES** at `99757bf4768e4ef558ea0548802b8435495b81ff`.

The `!v.repeat` change fixes the exact case I reported. But a no-op write that varies only its argument spelling still switches recovery off completely, and I reproduced that.

## What the delta fixes

**The exact probe2 cases now pass.** Same probe as before (`stagnationSteps: 30`, `maxRecoveries: 2`):

| Case | dcf2674c | 99757bf4 |
|---|---|---|
| identical no-op write alone | 30 steps, 0 notes | 5 steps; advisory, then constrained, then exhausted |
| identical no-op write + covered read | 31 steps, 62 calls run | 5 steps, 9 calls run, exhausted |
| reads only (control) | 7 steps, 3 refusals | 7 steps, 3 refusals |

**Variants that are closed:**
- **Alternating A/B content:** stops at 7 steps, exhausted, the same as 17addf0a. After the first cycle every write is a repeat.
- **Failed write** (missing `overwrite`): the classification is `NO_PROGRESS`, so it never counts. Stops at 5 steps, exhausted.
- **Write to a fresh path each step:** each write creates a genuinely new workspace state. `evaluateStep` counts that as progress, which was already true at 17addf0a, so nothing changed here.
- **Unknown workspace state:** `repeat` is always false there, so `ARTIFACT_MODIFIED` would always count. But on an unknown state, loops, coverage and refusals are all disabled anyway (`cli/src/core/execution_tracking.ts:497`, `:521`), as they were at 17addf0a. Only stagnation applies. This is a source check only; I could not easily produce an unknown state in a probe.

**The `except` parameter works.** At `cli/src/agent.ts:5649` the verdict being judged no longer counts for itself.

## Remaining finding (Medium): no-op writes with varied arguments

**Location:** `cli/src/agent.ts:5562`, used at `:5649` and `:5679`.

**Cause:** `repeat` is keyed on the action identity (tool name plus canonical arguments, `cli/src/core/execution_tracking.ts:266`), not on whether the workspace changed. Any change of spelling makes a new key, so the write is "not a repeat" and counts as progress. That suspends refusals and escalation for the whole step, every step. Meanwhile `evaluateStep` correctly sees an unchanged workspace and records no progress.

**Reproduction:** each step sends `[write_file(x.txt, same content), read_file(f.txt)]`, with `f.txt` already in context.

| Variant | 17addf0a | 99757bf4 |
|---|---|---|
| path spelled `./x.txt`, `././x.txt`, ... | 7 steps, 3 refusals, exhausted | 31 steps, 62 calls run, 0 notes, 0 refusals |
| same path, plus an extra argument `note: "n&lt;step&gt;"` | 7 steps, 3 refusals, exhausted | 31 steps, 62 calls run, 0 notes, 0 refusals |

**Impact:**
- Only `stagnationSteps` (default 12) stops the loop. Every redundant read in it runs instead of being refused.
- This is a regression against 17addf0a.
- It takes a model that changes how it spells a write which changes nothing, so it is less likely than the plain repeat. But it is exactly the kind of drift a stuck model shows.

**Suggested fix:** decide "successful change" from the workspace state, not from the action key. That would be the same test `evaluateStep` uses. Concretely: a mutation counts only if `inputStateFor()` after it differs from the state before it, and that state has not been seen before. Add a test with a spelling-varied no-op write.

## Subagent change

The subagent pairing is fine. `runSubagent` now calls `uniqueCallIds(parsed.toolCalls, tracker)` before its assistant push, with its own fresh tracker and the subagent's own history. Tool results, including the cancelled-before-execution path, use `call.id` after the push, on the same objects. Nothing reads the ids before the push.

## Legitimate case

Still works. "progress earlier in a step suspends refusal ... [new read, covered read, write] runs in full" passes at 99757bf4.

  - 8 cases at 99757bf4, all completed, with the results above.

<result>**Verdict: ACCEPT WITH NITS** at `f0dabf748ab5da21ac5c67db6bef489da70aadcf`, but 2 of 13 probe cases did not finish and I did not re-run the three test suites at this SHA (details below).

## Source check of the delta

- `progressedThisStep(except)` (`cli/src/agent.ts:5562-5568`) is true in two cases only. One is an earlier verdict that is `informative`. The other is an earlier `ARTIFACT_MODIFIED` verdict plus a current `inputStateFor()` that is not `"unknown"`, not `stepStateBefore`, and not in `tracker.data.seenStates`.
- This is the same test `endStep` applies through `evaluateStep` (`cli/src/core/execution_tracking.ts:748`). A write that changes nothing, however it is spelled, no longer counts.
- **`seenStates` is not updated mid-step.** The only writers are `evaluateStep` (`cli/src/core/execution_tracking.ts:748`, called from `endStep`) and `noteState` (`:782`, called at `cli/src/agent.ts:5500` and `:6695` at turn or subagent start). So the check cannot see a state added during the current step, and it cannot satisfy itself.
- **Oscillating states across steps:** `stepStateBefore` of step N+1 is the state already added by `endStep` of step N, so a return to an earlier state is never "new". This is confirmed by the oscillation probe below.

The fresh-path case is expected to reach `maxSteps` anyway, because each step is genuine progress and it is bounded by `stagnationSteps`. The shell-mutation case is the one I would most like to see finish. Its source logic should be safe: `run_command` is not in `MUTATORS`, so it never gets `ARTIFACT_MODIFIED` and cannot trigger the suspend path.

## Remaining bypass variants

- **Write then revert in one step (nit):** the intermediate write counted as new progress, so `recovery_progress_detected` was logged after `recovery_exhausted`. The turn still stopped at request 5 with `STAGNATION_DETECTED`, so nothing was bypassed. This matches `endStep`, because the step really did reach a new state.
- **Ignored paths:** no bypass. The `.git` write did not suspend anything.
- I found no variant that suspends recovery without changing the workspace.

## Over-strictness

The legitimate case still works. A real edit followed by a covered read in the same step, with the budget spent, ran both calls and returned to normal. The one behaviour change from 99757bf4 is that an edit counts as progress only when the workspace reached a state not seen before. That is the same bar `evaluateStep` uses, so it should not block real work.

Findings
Medium
Code review
·
6h ago
·
When candidate diff collection fails—for example, by exceeding the 4 MiB buffer at cli/src/core/operation_runner_execution.ts:45—this substitutes a short error message for the diff. The missing-evidence gate at cli/src/core/independent_review.ts:367–370 consequently permits a valid ACCEPT without passing verifier evidence because the replacement is below 20,000 characters. Make diff-collection failure block review completion instead of treating the placeholder as candidate evidence.

Code review
·
6h ago
·
Retrying a rejected review preserves agentTaskId (cli/src/core/scheduler.ts:417–424), so recovery unconditionally adds an unverified-write obligation (cli/src/agent.ts:1681–1684). The read-only reviewer cannot run verification (cli/src/agent.ts:4105–4108), so even a corrected ACCEPT returns UNVERIFIED and cannot complete. Retry with a fresh read-only task or otherwise prevent this path from inheriting that write-verification obligation.

Code review
·
12h ago
·
Non-isolated teams never populate dependency verification here because TeamOrchestrator.plan creates no Git isolation (cli/src/team.ts:83). For /team full --independent-review with a diff exceeding 20,000 characters, cli/src/core/independent_review.ts:367–370 therefore rejects every ACCEPT even when the implementer's verifier passed. Preserve and forward dependency verifier evidence for non-isolated operations too.

Code review
·
12h ago
·
The new specialist target support is unreachable through the interactive command: /team call_site_analyzer ... is rejected by cli/src/repl.ts:1600–1602, which still checks the five-entry TEAM_ROLES list at cli/src/team.ts:21. Update command target validation to include specialist roles.

Code review
·
12h ago
·
With authoritativeVerifierCommand configured, the new read-only specialist units cannot complete: cli/src/agent.ts:6176–6178 exempts only the original report roles, while lines 4015–4020 prevent read-only agents from running the verifier and lines 6391–6392 consequently return UNVERIFIED. Extend the completion-gate exemption to read-only specialist reports so valid results can complete.

Slop
·
12h ago
·
Verifier evidence only comes from integration receipts, and those exist only in isolated (git) operations. /team and other non-isolated runs therefore always build a package with verifierExitCode undefined. In parseIndependentReviewVerdict, unifiedDiff.length > 20000 && verifierExitCode !== 0 treats undefined as not passing, so in those modes every review of a diff larger than 20k characters is force-REJECTED and the operation blocks, whatever the reviewer says. The untracked-file diffs appended by reviewCandidateDiff make that size easy to reach. Either source the tester unit's verification in non-isolated runs, or don't treat missing evidence as a failure here.

Slop
·
12h ago
·
reviewEvidence sets verifierExitCode only when the dependency's verification is PASSED with executed tests. A failed verification becomes "status FAILED" prose with no exit code, so parseIndependentReviewVerdict's gate (verifierExitCode !== 0 → REJECT) can never fire, and a model ACCEPT goes through. review_unit_boundary.test.ts:110 hides this by putting verifierExitCode: 1 into the package by hand instead of building it from a FAILED receipt. EVIDENCE.md and ROADMAP still claim a failed verifier blocks the unit. Map a non-passing receipt to a non-zero code, and test it through reviewEvidence.

Code review
·
14h ago
·
For an in-scope file changing -- before to ++ after, Git emits consecutive hunk lines --- before and +++ after. This resets inHeader, adds before and after as filenames, and forces rejection of an otherwise valid change through cli/src/core/independent_review.ts:356–365. Track hunk boundaries before interpreting these pairs as file headers.

Code review
·
14h ago
·
An event such as {apiKey: "abcdef1234567890"} is persisted unchanged when the value is not separately registered as a secret: the replacer loses the key context required by cli/src/ui.ts:24. This exposes non-prefixed credentials in events.jsonl; atomicWriteJson at line 234 repeats the defect for other artifacts. Also redact the serialized structure, as cli/src/evidence.ts:42–44 already does.

Low
Slop
·
12h ago
·
The runtime package hard-codes authorizedScope: [], so detectScopeViolations always returns nothing and the reviewer is told the scope is "Unrestricted". The scope gate the docs list among the "deterministic gates" never runs in practice. operationId: ctx.objective.slice(0, 80) also puts the objective's text in the operation-ID slot. Pass the real operation ID and the unit's declared scope, or drop these fields from the runtime package instead of filling them with placeholders.

Slop
·
14h ago
·
The old JSDoc for extractModifiedFilesFromDiff ("Parses files modified from a unified git diff… git index lines") was left in place when the parser was rewritten. It now sits directly above readGitPath's own JSDoc, so it documents the wrong function and describes behavior (index lines) the parser doesn't have. Delete it.

## New findings
1. **Medium/high — committed work is invisible after a legacy resume.** `OperationRunner.recordStartCommit` (`cli/src/core/operation_runner.ts`, called from `run()` before `reconcileIntegrated`) sets `startCommit` to the current HEAD whenever it is missing, even if units already completed. Scenario: a non-isolated operation persisted before this change has the implementer COMPLETE and its commit made, with no `startCommit`. On resume, `startCommit` becomes the post-commit HEAD. The "no recorded base" block in `reviewEvidence` is skipped. My probe produced a diff of length 0 and the reviewer ACCEPT went through. Fix: record `startCommit` only when no unit has run, otherwise leave it unset so the block fires.
2. **Medium/low — a failing second dependency is ignored.** `reviewEvidence` uses `ctx.dependencies.find(d =&gt; d.verification)`, the first dependency with evidence (this line is unchanged from the previous head). Probe: dependencies `[PASSED, FAILED]` give ACCEPT, while `[FAILED, PASSED]` give REJECT. `/team full` gives the reviewer a single dependency, so it is reachable only through custom or CLI plans. Fix: take the worst result across all dependencies.
3. **Low — an empty diff with no evidence is accepted.** The probe produced `""` plus no verifier, and the parser returned ACCEPT. This is arguably legitimate for "no change needed", but it is the same outcome as finding 1.
4. **Low — scope-gate hole for plain diffs with inflated hunk counts** (item 7). Git-produced diffs always carry `diff --git` lines, and runtime packages declare no scope, so it is not reachable today.
5. **Low — evidence redaction by key name is incomplete (`cli/src/ceri/evidence_store.ts`, `SENSITIVE_KEY`).**
   - Probe leaks, unredacted: an array or object under a sensitive key (`{apiKey: ["..."]}`, `{token: {value: "..."}}`), plural keys (`credentials`, `secrets`), and `cookie`, `auth`, `bearer`, `jwt`.
   - Over-redaction: `{pass: 12}`, `{max_token: 4096}` and `bypass: "..."` become `[REDACTED]`, because the end-anchored regex matches them.
6. **Low — recovered read-only specialists stay stuck (`cli/src/agent.ts:1684`).** `researcher`, `semantic_explorer`, `call_site_analyzer` and `security_reviewer` keep `hasPendingModifications` after a retry. The turn-result code at about `agent.ts:6470` can then yield an unverified outcome for a read-only agent. This is consistent with the item 2 requirement, but the new specialists make the liveness cost real. I only read the code and did not reproduce it.
7. **Nit — option injection from persisted state.** `startCommit` and `baseCommit` come from persisted JSON and are passed to `git diff` as a positional argument with no `--end-of-options` or `^{commit}` validation. A value like `--output=...` could write a file. This needs write access to the local state directory, which the repo's own threat model already treats as untrusted. It is also an existing pattern for `baseCommit`.

<result>**Verdict: ACCEPT WITH NITS at 6fa72496cc0ea8ebbbddebdbc64221ab0af7f46b.** I found no blocking defects. Every R1–R8 fix works under my own probes, with two small regressions in R4 and R5 and a few wording or coverage nits.

**Setup**
- I used a new worktree at `/home/mike/nexus-wt/pr396-review-6fa72496`. HEAD is exactly 6fa72496 and origin/main is 9ed440a7.
- I symlinked `core/target` from `/home/mike/nexus-wt/pr396-review-bbcf7cc1/core/target` rather than rebuilding. `core/target/release/harness-core` exists (6.5M).
- `npm ci` and `npm run build` succeeded, and `npx tsc --noEmit -p .` is clean.
- I did not touch the other worktrees. `eval/` is not touched anywhere in `origin/main..HEAD`. I committed, pushed and posted nothing, and printed no secrets.

**Tests** (isolated HOME, one file at a time, all failures 0)
- **Required suites, all passing:**
  - review_unit_boundary 21
  - independent_review 12
  - specialist_roles_runtime 9
  - specialist_profiles 4
  - recovery_role_actions 4
  - team_convergence 47

  - operation_runner_refactor 3
  - operation_runner_restart 1
  - durable_continuity 42
- **Other suites passing:**
  - ceri_baseline 7, ceri_contracts 3, ceri_e2e 3, ceri_investigation 2, ceri_remediation 3
  - cloud_providers 44, compaction_continuity 34

  - approval_policy 15, capability_workspace_effects 3
- **Not run:** these suites import a changed module and the machine was heavily loaded (load average about 7.5), so I stopped after 32 files. I did not run them at the base either.
  - notification_gating, operation_account_identity, operation_concurrency, operation_git, operation_messages, operation_schema, operation_view
  - output_truncation_recovery, process_lifecycle, provider_recovery_agent, recover_worktree_conversation
  - spine, startup_trust_prompt, startup_unreachable_e2e, state, tool_groups, turn_semantics
  - verifier_gating, verifier_timeout, workspace_trust
- I made no base-comparison runs because nothing failed.

**R1: fixed.** I called `recordStartCommit` on fabricated states, so this tests the method directly rather than a full legacy-operation drive.
- A fresh operation with all-PENDING units sets the base.
- A first unit that is BLOCKED, PARKED or FAILED with runs=0 also sets the base. No work happened, so that is correct.
- A unit with COMPLETE or RUNNING (even with runs=0), runs&gt;0, or an agentTaskId leaves the base unset. So does any later unit having run.
- An isolated operation (`state.git` set) is unchanged: the method does not record there.
- A preset `startCommit` is untouched.
- Every unit that has actually started goes through `unit.runs++` in `scheduler.ts:500`, so I found no ran-but-unmarked path.
- The author's end-to-end legacy test passes: the review is BLOCKED with "starting commit" and `startCommit` stays undefined.

**R2: fixed.** Verified through `reviewEvidence`.
- [PASSED, FAILED] and [FAILED, PASSED] both give exit 1 with the failed command.
- [PASSED, PASSED] gives 0.
- A test-less PASSED, in either order, gives 1 with "(no tests executed)".
- TIMED_OUT, in either order, gives 1.
- Dependencies with no verification are skipped. If none have one, there is no verifier command or exit code.

**R3: fixed, with a wording nit.**
- A clean tree and an ignored-only change both block with "the candidate diff is empty".
- These produce a non-empty diff and do not block: untracked-only, an untracked empty file, binary-only, mode-only, rename-only, and a committed change with the recorded start base.
- Isolated operations diff against the operation base, so committed work is shown.
- The two edited tests only add an untracked `math.js` so the diff is non-empty. They keep their original assertions, so I judge them not weakened.
- Nit: the message reads "candidate diff unavailable: the candidate diff is empty", and the prompt tells the operator to "reduce the change", which is odd for an empty diff. The only option offered is retry.
- Nit: an operation whose implementer legitimately changed nothing now blocks its reviewer.

**R4: original bug still fixed, with one regression.**
- A `--- before` / `+++ after` pair inside a hunk gives only `["f"]` when followed by a context line, by `@@ not header` as context text, by an empty line, by `+@@`, or by `\ No newline at end of file`.
- A body line cannot start with `@@`: git prefixes every body line with space, `+`, `-` or `\`.
- An inflated hunk count followed by `--- a/secret`, `+++ b/secret`, `@@` now exposes `secret`. `detectScopeViolations` flags it.
- No file can be hidden through git's own output, because `diff --git` lines reset the counts.
- The regression is Low/Nit. A well-formed hunk whose last two body lines are a removed `-- before` and an added `++ after`, followed directly by another `@@` hunk, makes the new rule fire. It yields the false filenames `before` and `after`, so a harmless file looks out of scope. I reproduced this with real `git diff -U0` output (`["f.sql","before","after"]`), so it applies to the git-format path too. Default `git diff` (context 3) cannot produce it, because context lines always sit between hunks, and `reviewCandidateDiff` does not use -U0. In practice only hand-written diffs reach it. Scope is not declared at runtime today, so there is no runtime impact.
- Nit: an inflated count followed by a next-file `---`/`+++` pair with no `@@` after it still hides that file. Git cannot emit this, and the same fixture with `diff --git` lines is fine.

**R5: fixed, with a Low regression.** I tested about 120 key names and nested arrays end to end through `appendEvent`.
- Redacted: apiKey, api_key, API-KEY, accessToken, access_token, authToken, refreshToken, idToken, clientSecret, secret_access_key, privateKey, password, passwd, Authorization, set-cookie, x-api-key, bearer, jwt, session_token, tokenValue, bearerToken, githubToken, slackToken, a bare `token`, cookie, credentials, passphrase, oauthToken.
- Whole values are redacted for arrays and objects, including `[{"api_key":…}]` and `accessToken:{a:1}`.
- Kept as intended: tokens, max_tokens, input/output/total/prompt/completion tokens, cache_read_input_tokens, compass, passport, author, authors, pass, bypass, keyboard, key.
- Leaks, low severity. Because the matching is now by whole words, the single-lowercase-word and camelCase forms that the old suffix regex matched are no longer redacted. The old regex matched all of these (verified with old=true, new=false):
  - `accesstoken`, `refreshtoken`, `clientsecret`, `privatekey`
  - `DB_PASS` and `dbPass`, which the old regex matched via its `pass` suffix
  - `passWord` (camelCase splits to "pass word")
- Not matched, as before: `pat`, `sig`, `signature`, `pwd`, `creds`, `sshKey`, `encryptionKey`, `masterKey`, `licenseKey`.
- Over-redaction, harmless: `tokenCount`, `token_count` and `tokenLimit` are redacted.
- Fix: also test the whole lowercased key with the old suffix regex, or add concatenated lowercase forms to `SECRET_WORDS`.

**R6: fixed.** I ran resumed agents in three modes: default, explicit read-only, and `approvalMode` auto.
- Read-only specialists and `reviewer` resume with no pending obligation and outcome NOT_REQUIRED: semantic_explorer, call_site_analyzer, security_reviewer, acceptance_reviewer and reviewer. This holds even in auto mode, because the role itself forces read-only (`agent.ts:1107`).
- researcher, failure_debugger, tester, implementer and architect keep the obligation (pending=true, UNVERIFIED) in all three modes.

- `--output=&lt;path&gt;`, `-p`, `--stat`, an option followed by a newline and `--output=`, the empty string, `--end-of-options`, `HEAD; touch x`, `HEAD^{tree}`, `HEAD:a.txt` and a nonexistent branch all return an error.

<result>## Verdict: REQUEST CHANGES for 5fde750a

The focused tests pass, but I found one functional regression on the vLLM path and two real gaps in the other areas you asked about. I worked in detached worktrees (`~/nexus-wt/rv-wp1107` at 5fde750a, plus a temporary baseline at 3eea888a) with a throwaway HOME. I removed both worktrees at the end. I touched nothing under `eval/`; I copied the matrix files to the scratchpad for mutation tests. I did not print any token or secret.

## Findings, by severity

**HIGH: vLLM strict mode makes `write_file` and `run_command` fail at execution.**
- `cli/src/core/tool_grammar_provider.ts:35-41` turns every optional parameter into a required, nullable one. A strict-mode model will therefore emit `"overwrite": null`, `"background": null` and similar.
- The Rust core rejects those nulls. `core/src/file_ops.rs` declares `overwrite: bool`, and `core/src/runner.rs` declares `timeout_secs: u64`, `shell: String` and `background: bool`.
- I built the core from this commit and called `RustCoreBridge.executeTool` directly:
  - `write_file {overwrite:null}` returns `Invalid write_file arguments: invalid type: null, expected a boolean`.
  - `run_command` with `background:null` returns `Invalid run_command arguments: invalid type: null, expected a boolean`, and all-null fails the same way.
  - `timeout_secs`, `shell`, `cwd` and `service_name` as null each succeed on their own, as do `read_file` and `edit_file` nulls.
- Nothing in `client.ts` (around 1526 and 1792), `agent.ts` (`dispatchToolCallImpl`, around 4850) or the `executeTool` pipeline strips nulls before dispatch. I traced the agent path by reading it, not by running a full agent loop against a mock vLLM.
- The matrix would not catch this. It classifies a call as `native_valid` when it satisfies the adapted schema, and that schema allows null (`validateToolArguments` and its test at `tool_grammar_matrix.test.mjs:130`). A vLLM lane could score 100% while every `write_file` and `run_command` call fails when executed.
- The fix is to strip nulls for fields that were optional before the adapter ran. Do it in the client or the dispatch path, or make the core accept null.

**MEDIUM: REQ-PRV-109 holds only on the success path.**
- `routingHeaders()` (`client.ts:809-821`) suppresses X-Task-Class only when the cached kind is known. The commit now demotes the kind to `unknown` whenever a handshake fails, so the header is sent to a standalone server.
- I ran the same scenarios at base and head with a `taskClass:"subagent"` client:
  - llama.cpp marker with `/props` returning 404: base sent no header; head sent `X-Task-Class: subagent`.
  - Ollama with no `/api/version`, or with `/api/show` returning 404: header sent.
  - vLLM (`owned_by` vllm) with `/version` returning 404: header sent.
  - `NEXUS_SERVER_PROBE=0` with a llama.cpp marker: header sent.
  - After a successful identification, a rediscovery where `/models` returns 500 resets the kind to `unknown` (`serverKindCache.delete(ep)` at `client.ts:1139`), and the header is sent. This happens on the periodic refresh (`refreshCapabilities`, `CAPABILITY_TTL_MS`), whose comment says an unreadable catalog "keeps the last valid advertisement". The client and the agent now disagree on that.
  - In the same rediscovery case, vLLM also loses `strict` and the root `/tokenize` fallback.
- `test/task_class.test.ts` is unchanged and covers only the success path (llama.cpp marker with `/props` OK, then gateway).
- The fix is to decide the header from the catalog-level kind (`serverKindOf(entry)`), separately from handshake-verified adaptation. Also keep the previous kind when rediscovery returns `unavailable`.

**MEDIUM: the matrix runner does not enforce "only the verified installed candidate".**
- It checks a `.provenance.json` inside the caller-supplied `installRoot` (`eval/tool_grammar_matrix.mjs:259-270`). That file is self-attested JSON. Nothing ties it to the files under `lib/cli/dist`, the archive contents, or `MANIFEST.sha256`. `install.sh` checks the manifest at install time, but the runner never does.
- I demonstrated three bypasses against a copy of the runner:
  - A forged `.provenance.json`, a hand-written `lib/cli/dist` and an unrelated "archive" file were accepted: `passed: true`, with the archive's SHA recorded.
  - A symlinked `lib` pointing outside `installRoot` ran the outside runtime. `path.resolve` is used and there is no `realpath`.
  - Swapping the `lib` symlink right after the provenance read ran the swapped runtime (verify-then-import TOCTOU).
- The hand-written fixture in the committed test is itself this pattern.
- A caller-supplied `runtime` or `tools` option is rejected (own property), and an inherited `runtime` is never read. Env vars cannot substitute the runtime. `NEXUS_SERVER_PROBE=0` fails closed with a "provider identity mismatch" error. A wrong pinned `archiveSha256` is rejected.
- The regression test is genuinely RED without the guard. With the `Object.hasOwn(options,"runtime")` line removed, the test fails with `Missing expected rejection` (9 pass, 1 fail). But the guard is cosmetic, because the option is never consumed.
- The fix is to realpath `installRoot`, verify `MANIFEST.sha256` plus the archive-to-install link, and verify once and import from the same resolved path.
- The evidence fields `serverVersion`, `imageDigest` and `modelDigest` are config-attested. They are not compared with what the server reports. I did not check whether the plan intends that.

**MEDIUM-LOW: the vLLM identity check is weak.**
- `vllmCatalogMarker` is true for any `max_model_len` or `context_length` entry, because `parseAdvertisement` sets `via:"vllm"` for those (`provider_capabilities.ts:122`).
- I tested a catalog with only `max_model_len` and no `owned_by`, plus a `/version` response: kind became `vllm` and `strict:true` was sent.
- Any non-vLLM server with a `/version` route would be misidentified. I believe SGLang exposes one, but I did not verify that.
- The commit's own test (`client_tool_grammar.test.ts:51`) uses this shape, so it is by design, but it is weaker than the "generic catalog stays unknown" wording in the plan. Requiring `owned_by` vllm would be stricter.

**LOW and nits:**
- The WP-1107-PLAN AC4 test names do not exist in the repo. Missing are "client omits constrained fields for unknown and unsupported providers", "generic context catalog remains unknown…", and the four "provider identity accepts…" names. Substance is partly covered by other tests.
- `identityCached` at `client.ts:1156` is effectively dead, because the cache is deleted at line 1139.
- `docs/LOCAL_SERVERS.md` is unchanged. It still says vLLM is read directly from `max_model_len` and lists only the old probes under `NEXUS_SERVER_PROBE`.
- `adaptToolDefinitionsForServer` throws a TypeError if an allowlisted tool name ever has an optional array or object parameter. That would break every vLLM request instead of falling back. It is unreachable with the current registry.
- An Ollama `/api/show` response whose `model` field differs from the configured name (for example `:latest`) demotes the endpoint to unknown. I did not verify whether real Ollama sends that field.
- The new probes send the same origin-scoped credential as the existing `/props` probe, with `redirect:"error"`. I found no new leakage.
- Tool names and schemas are never placed into grammar text in this commit. The adapter works on JSON, restricted to the allowlisted names.
- It runs after the egress sanitization step, and unknown, llama.cpp, Ollama, gateway and LM Studio kinds return the same array reference unchanged.

## Q1: changed fixtures and assertions
I ran the 3eea888a test files against the 5fde750a source. Only three old assertions fail, and all three are legitimate updates to the new identity rule. None hides a regression by itself:
- **Ollama (`server_context_discovery`):** the old test fails with `via 'catalog'` instead of `'ollama'`, because there is no `/api/version` and the kind is now unknown. The update adds `GET /api/version` and expects it in the request sequence.
- **vLLM (`server_context_discovery`):** the old test fails only on the request sequence (an extra `GET /version`). Context discovery still returns 16384 (probe case 7, which has `owned_by` vllm but no `/version`).
- **`tokens`:** the old test fails with `HTTP 404`, because an unidentified vLLM no longer gets the root `/tokenize` URL. This is a real consequence of the rule, but real vLLM answers `/version`.
- **`standalone_profiles`:** the old test passes against the new source, and the change only adds a detected-kind assertion, so it is strengthened.
- **`model_proxy`:** only adds `/version` and `/api/version` routes for profile emulation.
- **`provider_capabilities` and the matrix wrapper:** additions only.
- **The 33 failures in `/tmp/wp1107-full-gate-final4.log`:** the ones I checked were host or environment issues (unbuilt `nexus-tui`, mount allowlist paths). Three were the identity-rule tests above, plus the Ollama standalone profile and a standalone server-profile run test (`eval_dataset`; I did not read its failure detail).
- **Coverage lost:** nothing asserts "context still discovered for a vLLM catalog without `/version`", and the vLLM discovery test does not assert the detected kind.
- **Coupling:** Ollama context discovery is now coupled to the `/api/version` handshake, so an Ollama proxy that exposes `/api/show` but not `/api/version` loses its context. This is a deliberate-looking tightening, but it goes beyond what the plan requires.

## Not verified

- The packaged installer layout. I checked only that real installed trees (v1.1.1) have `lib/cli/dist/{client,parser,core/capabilities}.js`, a `.provenance.json` with a 40-character `revision`, and `MANIFEST.sha256`.
- Whether `NEXUS_SERVER_PROBE` from `config.env` reaches `process.env`.
