<result># Independent review: WP-1105 deterministic code evaluators as of 5fde750a

**Verdict: REQUEST CHANGES.** The sandbox containment is solid, and the telemetry counts each call once, including subagents. But `typecheck_clean` can pass without measuring anything, and the `syntax_ast` default can fail honest runs. I'd fix F1–F3 before relying on these evaluators for scores.

Setup: detached worktree `~/nexus-wt/rv-wp1105` at 5fde750a, isolated HOME/XDG, `cli/node_modules` copied from main. Main's lockfile differs slightly from this commit's. The worktree is removed. No push, post or edit in any checkout. Mutations ran in non-git copies under the scratchpad.

## Test counts
- **`code_evaluators.test.ts`:** 22 tests, 22 pass, 0 fail.
- **`parser_safety.test.ts`:** 12 pass. **`parser_single_quote.test.ts`:** 10 pass.
- **`eval/dataset.test.mjs`:** 12 pass. **`eval/package_runtime.test.mjs`:** 2 pass, 3 skipped, 0 fail.
- **`npx tsc --noEmit`:** exit 0.
- **`eval_dataset.test.ts`:** 1 test, failed. Its output says "compiler exposure could not be checked; refusing qualification". The run was refused before any evaluator ran, so I read it as a host-environment limit, not an evaluator defect.

## Findings, ranked by severity

**F1 (MEDIUM): `typecheck_clean` passes vacuously when the checker cannot run on either side.**
- Evidence: `typecheck_clean.mjs:41-49` tolerates any failing baseline as long as the candidate adds no new diagnostic lines.
- I ran the real sandbox with `node ./node_modules/typescript/bin/tsc` and no node_modules in either workspace. The result was `passed:true, score:1`, with 12 identical "Cannot find module" lines on both sides (`vacuous.mjs`).
- It also passed when the candidate had a syntax error the checker never saw.
- A bwrap setup failure that exits 1 with the same stderr on both sides passes the same way.
- Suggested fix: require the baseline to be a recognised state, for example exit 0 or a task-supplied diagnostic regex such as `error TS\d+`.

**F2 (MEDIUM): `build` and `typecheck_clean` run candidate-modified build config, so a candidate can game them.**
- Evidence: `run.mjs:308` passes `candidateWorkspace: ws`, the agent-modified tree. Hardened mode's `configChanges` refusal applies only to the hidden and regression checks.
- With real tsc, a candidate that rewrote `tsconfig.json` to `"files":["empty.ts"]` while keeping a new error file got `passed:true`, 0 candidate diagnostics (`gaming.mjs`).
- A candidate that honestly added a second error was correctly failed.
- Mitigations: pin the config from the baseline, or require `diff_boundary` to cover config files. The README doesn't say this.

**F3 (MEDIUM): `syntax_ast` fed with the runner's default file list false-fails honest agents.**
- Evidence: `runner.mjs:162` sets `files = spec.files ?? changedFiles`, which includes deleted and non-source paths.
- A scenario with an edited `src/a.ts`, a deleted `src/old.ts` and an edited `README.md` gave `passed:false`. The failures were `README.md: unsupported source extension` and `src/old.ts: ENOENT`.
- Any task that doesn't set `spec.files` can never pass if the agent deletes a file or edits a non-code file.

**F4 (LOW-MEDIUM): evaluator commands have no memory, pid or disk limits.**
- Evidence: `command.mjs` calls `runProcess(spec, {outputLimit})` with no `resources`, so `spawnContained` takes its uncontained branch (`resources.mjs:115`).
- Inside the sandbox:
  - `/proc/self/cgroup` is the user's session scope.
  - `/tmp` is a 24 GB tmpfs (about half of RAM).
  - `/workspace` is the real disk.
  - 300 background sleepers started with no cap (304 pids visible).
- Only wall time (default 120 s) and a 4 MiB output cap bound a candidate fork bomb or memory hog. Agent runs under run.mjs get a cgroup contract; the evaluators don't.
- I did not run an actual bomb, because the host is shared.

**F5 (LOW-MEDIUM): in-process parsers have no timeout.**
- `parseTypeScript` calls `stripTypeScriptTypes` synchronously inside the harness process.
- A 65 MB `.ts` file of repeated `const a = 1;` parsed in 62.9 s, blocking the event loop. The snapshot cap allows up to 256 MiB.
- Deeply nested input is handled safely: stack overflow is caught and reported as a failure.
- Python and JS parsing run in child processes with a 30 s timeout. They run on the host, not in bwrap, but only parse and never execute.

**F6 (LOW): a command path is not confined to the workspace or system directories.**
- `resolveCommand` accepts a relative path and takes its `realpath`.
- A workspace symlink `tool.sh -&gt; /tmp/rv-outside/hostscript.sh` ran the host script (`HOSTSCRIPT-RAN`). A `../` command traversal did the same.
- A host executable without a shebang leaked its second line through sh's ENOEXEC fallback: stderr said `data.txt: 2: line2: not found`.
- That stderr lands in `typecheck_clean.newDiagnostics` in the results record. It does not go back to the agent, and the command comes from trusted task config, so exposure is small.
- Fix: refuse a non-system command whose `realpath` leaves the workspace.

**F7 (LOW): `typecheck_clean` false-fails on benign changes.**
- Inserting a comment line above a pre-existing error shifts `index.ts(1,14)` to `(2,14)`, which is reported as a new diagnostic (real tsc, `gaming.mjs`).
- A changed summary line such as `Found 3 errors` to `Found 2` does the same.
- This fails closed, and the README only says to normalise paths.

**F8 (NIT):**
- Telemetry skips calls to tools that weren't offered or are malformed. The legacy text-parsing path (`NEXUS_TEXT_TOOL_PARSING=legacy`) doesn't count at all, so `json_repair_rate` reports "no tool calls recorded".
- The `hiddenWorkspace` argument to `runCodeEvaluators` is unused.
- Root `.git` changes are invisible to `diff_boundary`. Overwriting `.git/HEAD` still passed. This is documented, and run.mjs does not run git on the candidate afterwards.

## 1. Runner security: what held
- **argv:** I fed it `--bind / /x`, `$(id)` and `;id`, and they arrived as literal argv. NUL bytes and non-string args are rejected, and `timeoutMs` of 0, -1, 1.5, "5", null, Infinity and NaN is rejected.
- **No shell, no host fallback:** the argv goes to `/usr/bin/bwrap` after `--` and the process runs with `env:{}`. The "fails closed" test passes, and removing the sandbox check fails that test.
- **Environment:** the sandbox saw only `PATH, HOME, LANG, PWD`. Caps are 0 and NoNewPrivs is 1. The uid is the same user (`id` showed `mike`), so this is namespace isolation, not a separate account.
- **Symlink escapes:**
  - Symlinked directories and files inside the workspace to host paths returned ENOENT.
  - A symlink to `/etc/shadow` returned ENOENT.
  - `/proc/1/root` showed only the sandbox root.
  - The host secret path and `/home` showed only `eval`.
  - A symlinked `cwd` resolved to `/workspace`.
- **Output bound:** a 25 MB write was killed with "exceeded the output limit" (stdout stopped at 4.16 MB). A 5 MB stderr write hit the same error.
- **Timeout and cleanup:**
  - A process that traps TERM, plus a `setsid` descendant sleeping 2 s then writing `late.txt`, was stopped at 724 ms with timeoutMs 700.
  - `late.txt` never appeared, and the sleepers were gone.
- **Snapshot:** it uses `O_NOFOLLOW`, device and inode checks, and rejects special files (FIFO tested). Symlink loops fail closed, and `syntax_ast` rejects absolute paths, `..`, symlink escapes and a FIFO.

## 2. `typecheck_clean` rule: still correct as designed
I re-ran an 18-row case table (`table.mjs`). All the intended outcomes held:
- Silent non-zero exit fails.
- Fewer diagnostics with a different non-zero exit passes.
- Baseline 0 and candidate non-zero fails.
- Duplicate lines are counted.
- Null status, a signal, a timeout and an overflow error all fail.

The rule has the F1 and F7 blind spots above. "Fewer diagnostics with a different exit passes" also matches how a legitimate partial fix behaves.

## 3. Test quality: 16 mutations
**Killed (real tests):**
- Removing the sandbox availability check.
- Removing `--unshare-net`, which made both containment tests fail with exit 73.
- Binding host `/tmp` read-write.
- Removing `--unshare-pid` (the descendant test failed).
- Removing `--die-with-parent` (the descendant test failed).
- Making the `.git` skip apply at any depth.
- Dropping the silent-failure clause of the typecheck rule, dropping the regression term from `passed`, and making the typecheck rule strictly compare exit codes.
- The `diff_boundary` prefix check (`startsWith(prefix)` without the slash).
- Ignoring `invalid` in `json_repair_rate`.
- The `syntax_ast` escape check.
- Dropping both the `O_NOFOLLOW` and the inode check together (either one alone survives, so they are redundant).
- Telemetry:
  - Dropping the subagent roll-up.
  - Dropping the count in the subagent loop.
  - Dropping the count in the main loop.
  - Counting the roll-up twice.

**Survived (test gaps):**
- Dropping `baselineResult.status === 0` from the regression rule. This needs a baseline exit 0 with a diagnostic line the candidate repeats while exiting non-zero.
- Weakening the escape check to `startsWith(rootReal)` without the separator, so a sibling directory like `/tmp/ws-evil` is untested.
- Removing the root `.git` skip.
- Removing the per-turn reset of `turnToolCallSyntax` (`agent.ts:5546`). Two `runTurn` calls on one agent are not tested.
- No test covers output-limit overflow, command-symlink confinement, or the `syntax_ast` default list with deleted or non-source files.

## 4. Telemetry
- **One count per call:** every call is counted once in `parser.ts`. Native calls count after the name check. The XML tag, fenced and invalid-tag paths are mutually exclusive, so there is no double count.
- **Where it is counted:** the main loop (`agent.ts:6099`) and the subagent loop (`:6972`) each count once per model response.
- **Subagent roll-up:** once, in the `finally` at `:4548`. Nested subagents roll up recursively through the same handler.
- **Check:** I ran the real `nexus run --json` against a gateway sending 2 calls (one with a trailing comma). It emitted `turn.toolCallSyntax = {native:1,trailing:1,structural:0,invalid:0}`.
- `projectTurn` keeps the field.
- Truncated replies are dropped before parsing, per the README.

## 5. Not verified
- The harness end to end with a real task: `eval_dataset.test.ts` is blocked by a host compiler-exposure check.
- I did not run `eval_hardened`, `eval_integrity`, `eval_harness`, `tool_grammar_matrix`, or `parser_*` beyond the two files above.
- An actual fork bomb, memory exhaustion or disk fill inside the sandbox.
- Whether the agent's `--json` stdout could be spoofed by candidate output, which would affect the `json_repair_rate` input.
- The README's Node-version claim for `stripTypeScriptTypes`: I only ran Node v24.11.1.
- `isolate.mjs` internals such as compiler-exposure masking, which were not in the diff range.
- `agent.ts` changes outside the telemetry hunks (about 350 lines of other work).
- Whether the 5fde750a change to `model_proxy.mjs` affects evaluators.
- The dependency delta between this commit's lockfile and main's `node_modules`.</result>

<result>**Verdict on b032791e: REQUEST CHANGES.** Findings 1, 3 and 4 are fixed. Finding 2 is only partly fixed: the header leak is closed, but the server kind is still dropped on an unreadable rediscovery.

I built the Rust core in the worktree, drove `AutonomousAgent.dispatchToolCall` against the real `harness-core`, and removed the worktree at the end. My scratch scripts are in `/tmp/claude-1000/-home-mike-nexus-code/ee800dc3-6bb6-4e55-89e6-d6c2e85af4b6/scratchpad/`.

## Status of the four findings

**1. HIGH, null optional args: FIXED**
- Every optional field of the four allowlisted tools reaches the real core without error, whether passed alone as null or all together. Fields:
  - read_file: start_line, end_line, offset, length
  - write_file: overwrite
  - edit_file: replace_all
  - run_command: cwd, timeout_secs, shell, background, service_name
- The agent only strips `write_file.overwrite` and `run_command.background`, `timeout_secs` and `shell`. The other nulls are forwarded and the Rust side accepts them because those fields are `Option&lt;_&gt;`. This relies on core types and a hardcoded TS list staying in sync.
- Required fields given as null are still rejected before dispatch (FAIL-002) for path, content, old_content, new_content and command. Nothing is defaulted and nothing is written.
- Null and explicit values are told apart correctly:
  - `overwrite:null` on an existing file is refused (default false), the same as `false`, and `true` overwrites.
  - `replace_all:null` on an ambiguous edit is refused, and `true` replaces both occurrences.
  - `content:""`, `shell:""`, `cwd:""` and `timeout_secs:0` are kept and not stripped.
- All model-originated dispatch goes through `dispatchToolCall` (3 call sites).
- Mutation: with the strip block removed, `vllm_nullable_dispatch.test.ts` fails with "invalid type: null, expected a boolean".

**2. MEDIUM, REQ-PRV-109 X-Task-Class: PARTIAL**

I ran the same scenarios against 7f915894 (extracted to scratch) and b032791e. "Known" below means the kind had been detected before the rediscovery.

| Scenario | 7f915894 header | b032791e header |
|---|---|---|
| llama.cpp `/props` 404 | sent | suppressed |
| Ollama no `/api/version` | sent | suppressed |
| Ollama `/api/show` 404 | sent | suppressed |
| vLLM `/version` 404 | sent | suppressed |
| `NEXUS_SERVER_PROBE=0` (llama.cpp, Ollama, vLLM) | sent | suppressed |
| bare catalog, or no `/models` at all | sent | suppressed |
| gateway, normal discovery | sent | sent |
| rediscovery `/models` 503, bad JSON or dropped connection, known llama.cpp/Ollama/vLLM | sent | suppressed |
| same, known gateway | sent | sent (kind retained) |

- **Not fixed:** a known llama.cpp, Ollama or vLLM endpoint falls to kind `unknown` on those unreadable-catalog rediscoveries, and also on `/models` 404 and "model not listed". For vLLM that means `strict` is lost and the `/tokenize` root fallback is lost. Identical at 7f915894 and b032791e.
- The agent keeps the last advertisement (`via: vllm`) and still tries token counting, so the two disagree.
- The plan's AC8 deliberately narrows retention to gateway only. That is narrower than what you asked.
- Gateway returning 404 or "not listed" also drops its kind, so the header is lost.
- Mutation: the new task_class tests do fail without the fix:
  - Reverting `client.ts` and `provider_capabilities.ts` gives 8 fails.
  - Reverting only `routingHeaders` gives 5 fails.
  - Reverting rediscovery to delete at start gives 1 fail.

**3. MEDIUM, matrix runner: FIXED**
- Rejected, on a scratch copy of the original runner:
  - forged provenance with handwritten dist and an unrelated archive
  - a forged installed MANIFEST
  - symlinked `lib/`
  - symlinked installRoot, or a symlink in its parent path
  - an installed file symlinked to the archive's file
  - archives containing a symlink or hardlink
  - an extra installed file
  - a wrong pinned digest
  - caller `runtime` or `tools` options
  - a provenance revision mismatch
- Swapping the installed `client.js`, the installRoot directory (to a symlink), or the archive file after verification had no effect. Only the verified private snapshot ran.
- Real rc.11 archives contain only regular files and directories, so the strict type check will not reject them.
- Mutation on the regression test:
  - It fails when the post-snapshot verification call is removed, when the import is taken from the install root, or when the per-file digest compare is removed.
  - It does not fail when the provenance check, the installed-symlink check, the realpath check, the file-set comparison or the package-tree digest compare is removed. Those are defence in depth, since the runtime is imported from the snapshot, but they are untested.

**4. MEDIUM-LOW, vLLM identity: FIXED**
- A catalog with only `max_model_len` or `context_length` plus a `/version` route is now `unknown` with `strict` false (it was `vllm` with `strict` true at 7f915894).
- A real vLLM response (`owned_by:"vllm"`, `max_model_len`, a non-empty `/version`) is still `vllm`. `VLLM` in any case works. A blank version gives `unknown`.
- Behaviour change: `owned_by:"vllm"` with `/version` OK but no limits is now `vllm`; it was `unknown`. The old test asserting the "context handshake" requirement was replaced to match.

No assertion was weakened silently. The reversed expectations match the stated behaviour change:
- Header-sent expectations became header-absent in `task_class.test.ts` and `client_robustness.test.ts`.
- The vLLM `via` assertion changed to `catalog`.
- The old "owner marker without limits stays unknown" test was replaced by a broader one.

## New findings, ranked

1. **MEDIUM (finding 2 remainder):** the unreadable-`/models` rediscovery drops the kind for non-gateway servers, as described above.
2. **LOW-MEDIUM:** the null stripping only touches the dispatch copy. Read-coverage and observation tracking still read the raw call arguments. Under vLLM strict, `readRequests("read_file", {path, offset:null, length:null, …})` returns `undefined` where an omitted-field call returns a request. The same happens for a shell read with `cwd:null`, and `observationKey` differs. So read tracking is silently degraded for strict-mode calls. Normalizing at the parse step would fix this and make the hardcoded per-tool list unnecessary.
3. **LOW:** a transient `/version` failure on a successful rediscovery downgrades vLLM to `unknown` and loses strict.
4. **LOW:** in the matrix runner, `sourceCommit` is only compared with `.provenance.json`, which is excluded from hashing. It is not checked against the archive's own `compatibility.json`. `archiveSha256` pinning is optional, so an attacker-built archive with a matching install runs. The digest is recorded in the evidence, so the downstream gate must pin it.
5. **NIT:** the new `vllm_nullable_dispatch` test only exercises `write_file.overwrite` and the three `run_command` fields. The `read_file` and `edit_file` cases and the required-null rejection are untested.

## Not verified

- The full test suite, `make test`, and other test files (you reserved the host).
- Live provider inference against a real vLLM, llama.cpp or Ollama; all servers were mocks.
- The real installer (`install.sh`) producing an install tree that passes the runner end to end. I only checked its provenance fields and the rc.11 archive entry types.
- First-request header behaviour on a real gateway before capability discovery has run.
- Approval or authority behaviour with null `cwd` in restrictive modes (I ran only `fullAccess` and auto-approve); I did read `authority.ts`, where a null `cwd` is falsy and skipped.
- A runtime that loads a `node_modules` outside the archive. Real archives ship `lib/cli/node_modules` and it is covered by the manifest.
- A same-uid attacker with write access to the snapshot directory between verification and import.
- Rust-side behaviour of non-integer numbers for usize/u64 fields.
- The `harness-core` build is a release build at this commit; I did not diff it against a prior build.</result>

## Status per finding (all runs are at 0d506762)

**F1 (typecheck passed vacuously): FIXED at 0d506762, but 05913af5 probably reopens it.**
- Run with the real sandbox and real `tsc`:
  - No `node_modules` on either side now gives `infrastructureFailure` and `passed:false`.
  - A candidate syntax error with no `node_modules` also fails.
  - With `node_modules` present, the candidate's syntax error appears as a new TS1109 diagnostic.
- Unit cases: identical "Cannot find module" lines on both sides fail, and so do identical `bwrap:` setup failures.
- The gate is a hard-coded `error TS\d+:` regex, not the "task-supplied diagnostic regex" you described. Checkers that do not print TS codes (eslint, mypy, `cargo check`) always fail when the baseline already has errors. That fails closed but is undocumented. `eval/evaluators/code/README.md` and `SPEC-EVALUATION` still say `cargo check` is supported and still describe the old rule.
- Residual gap: identical recognised config errors on both sides, such as TS5057 (no tsconfig found), still pass vacuously. Low severity.
- 05913af5 changes the regex to `\berror(?:\s+TS\d+|\[E\d+\])?:` with the `/i` flag. The code group is now optional, so any line containing `error:` counts as recognised. That includes Node's `Error: Cannot find module '...'` and probably many sandbox errors.
- If so, F1 scenario 1 (no `node_modules`, identical lines on both sides) goes back to `passed:true`. This is from reading the diff, **not verified (host reserved)**. The fix is to require a Rust code (`error\[E\d+\]:`) or a TS code, not an optional one. The new test should include "Error: Cannot find module" on both sides.

**F2 (candidate-modified build config): PARTIAL.**
- Now blocked (real tsc):
  - `tsconfig.json` rewritten to `"files":["empty.ts"]`.
  - A new error file with `"include":["nothing"]`.
  - `tsconfig.build.json`.
  - An `extends` chain through `config/base.json`.
  - `package.json` scripts: a candidate with `"build":"true"` still fails because the baseline package.json is used.
  - A `scripts/build.js` rewrite.
  - `.tsbuildinfo` incremental state (the new diagnostic was still seen).
  - A patched `node_modules/typescript/bin/tsc` (no extension, so not overlaid).
- Still open:
  - **node_modules shadowing.** Rewriting `node_modules/typescript/lib/tsc.js` to `process.exit(0)` while keeping a type error gave `build` and `typecheck_clean` both `passed:true`. The overlay copies every candidate-changed `.ts/.js/.rs/.py`, `node_modules` included.
  - **Any other in-repo build helper** is overlaid as "source". `tools/check.js` rewritten to `exit(0)` made `build` pass. The same applies to `build.rs`, `setup.py`, `conftest.py` and the like. Only `*.config.*` and `scripts/{build,compile,typecheck}.*` are excluded.
- The honest candidate who legitimately edits `tsconfig.json` or `package.json` is not wrongly failed on source checks. A new `.d.ts` and a tsconfig edit that includes a new `lib/` dir both passed. The edited config is ignored silently, though, and that is not documented. Candidate config changes are neither reported nor failed.

**F3 (syntax_ast default list): FIXED, with one pre-existing exception.**
- Edit `src/a.ts`, delete `src/old.ts`, edit `README.md`: passes, and `files` is only `src/a.ts`.
- Still failing as required:
  - A broken `.ts` edit.
  - A broken `.TS`.
  - A broken `.ts` while another file is renamed to `.txt`.
  - A symlink to `/etc/passwd`.
  - A dangling `.ts` symlink.
- Passing as intended: only-README edited, only-deletion, a directory named `foo.ts`.
- An explicit `files` list that includes a deleted path still fails with ENOENT, which is the old behaviour.
- A broken file renamed to `.txt` is skipped. That is inherent to the design.
- Pre-existing, not in this commit: `node --check` returns 0 on ESM-syntax `.js` such as `export const a = ;` under Node v24.11.1, so syntax errors in ambiguous `.js` files pass. This is a false pass in `parsers.mjs`.

**Lower items.**
- **F4 (memory/pid/disk limits): untouched.** `command.mjs`, `isolate.mjs` and `parsers.mjs` have no diff.
- **F5 (parser timeout, 65 MB .ts blocking 62.9 s): untouched.** The TS parser is still in-process with no timeout, and the Node and Python child checks keep their 30 s timeout.
- **F6 (command-path symlink or `..` escape): untouched.** `resolveCommand` is unchanged.
- **F7 (exact-line comparison, false-fails on line shifts): untouched.** A row with line numbers shifted by one still fails.

## 18-row typecheck rule table
- I compared the old and new `typecheck_clean` with a fake runner. All the rules you listed hold.
  - Silent non-zero fails.
  - Fewer diagnostics with a different non-zero exit passes.
  - Baseline 0 plus candidate non-zero fails.
  - Duplicates are counted.
  - A crash or timeout fails.
- Only three rows differ from before. Identical non-TS failures on both sides, identical bwrap failures, and non-TS baseline errors (eslint-style) went from pass to fail.
- `statusRegression` is now effectively dead.
- The 0d506762 table was not re-run against 05913af5.

## New findings, ranked

1. **HIGH, fail-open.** `build` and `typecheck_clean` now run in baseline plus an overlay of only `.ts/.mts/.cts/.tsx/.js/.mjs/.cjs/.jsx/.rs/.py` candidate files.
   - Edits to any other file are invisible to the build: `.go`, `.c`, `.java`, `.json`, `.css`, `.vue`, `.sh`.
   - Reproduced with a `.go` edit that breaks the build and a new `.c` file that breaks it: both gave `build passed:true, status 0`. A `.ts` control failed correctly.
   - The same cause makes honest candidates fail. A new `src/data.json` imported with `resolveJsonModule` gave a false TS2307 and failed.
   - This regresses the previous behaviour, where `build` ran in the candidate workspace. It should fail closed for unsupported changed files, or use a deny-list overlay rather than an allow-list.
2. **MEDIUM.** The `node_modules` and build-helper overlay vectors listed under F2.
3. **MEDIUM, 05913af5.** The optional-code regex described under F1 likely reopens the vacuous pass.
4. **MEDIUM, honest-run failure.**
   - The runner now takes two full workspace snapshots, baseline and candidate, even for build-only tasks.
   - A ~54k-file `node_modules` gave `workspace snapshot refused: traversal deadline exceeded` after 5 s, so `build` failed.
   - The 50,000-entry cap would also refuse it.
   - Single runs took 10–25 s with a 23 MB `typescript` copy.
   - A candidate that adds a dependency under `node_modules` fared differently: the missing-module case was not isolated, and the left-pad probe passed only because it is a `.d.ts`. I am not claiming that outcome is correct.
5. **LOW.**
   - The pinned workspace is created under `os.tmpdir()`, not under `config.resources.workspaceRoot`.
   - A candidate symlink with a source extension (`src/link.ts`) fails all of `build` and `typecheck_clean` with "not a stable regular file".
   - `spec.candidate.cwd` is silently overridden by the pinned root. In a subdirectory test the candidate ran at the root while the explicit baseline kept its subdirectory.
   - With an explicit `baseline` but no `typecheckBaselineWorkspace`, `typecheck_clean` now errors where it used to run.
   - `README.md` is stale on the `typecheck_clean` rule, the pinned workspace and the `syntax_ast` default list.
   - `--pretty` ANSI output defeats the TS regex and fails closed.

<result>**Verdict for the WP-1107 half of 0d506762: REQUEST CHANGES** (borderline between that and ACCEPT WITH NITS). Per the later instruction I stopped running anything after the second message, and I removed `~/nexus-wt/rv-wp1107c` and `~/nexus-wt/rv-wp1107c-base`. The rest of this report comes from runs I had already finished and from code reading.

**Where I ran things**
- Everything ran from `~/nexus-wt/rv-wp1107c`, from the base worktree `rv-wp1107c-base` (b032791e, which I added for comparison), or from scratch copies under my session scratchpad.

**Finding status**

**A. REQ-PRV-109 retention: PARTIAL.**
- Fixed part. I ran the scenario table (4 kinds × 8 rediscovery modes) at both commits.
  - At b032791e a 503, bad JSON, dropped connection, 404 or missing model dropped llama.cpp, Ollama and vLLM to `unknown`, which also lost vLLM strict and the `/tokenize` fallback.
  - At 0d506762 all of these keep the kind, vLLM keeps `strict:true` and the root `/tokenize` fallback, and `X-Task-Class` stays absent for standalone servers.
  - A readable generic catalog correctly clears the kind to `unknown`.
- Not fixed, staleness. There is no bound on retention. `serverKindCache` is a process-wide map keyed only by endpoint string, with no TTL, no failure counter and no invalidation. The commit removed every `delete`.
  - A vLLM kind survived 3 further 503s.
  - It survived a swap to a llama.cpp server that did not list the model.
  - A fresh client for the same endpoint inherits the old kind before any discovery. That inheritance was already true before the commit.
  - Only a readable catalog entry for the selected model refreshes it. A replaced server that returns 404, 503 or a catalog without the model leaks the old `strict` flag.
  - I did not find any invalidation on endpoint switch, `/model` provider switch or account change; I grepped for it.
- Gateway behaviour is not unchanged. At b032791e a gateway whose `/models` returned 404, or whose catalog did not list the model, went to `unknown` and `X-Task-Class` was suppressed. At 0d506762 it stays `gateway` and the header is sent. In my scenario "gateway replaced by a standalone server returning 404 on `/models`" the header was `subagent`, so it leaks to a non-gateway server. A 503 or dropped connection behaved the same in both commits.

**B. Null normalisation: PARTIAL.**
- Fixed part:
  - The old per-tool strip list is gone from `agent.ts`. It is replaced by the schema-driven `normalizeNullableOptionalToolArguments`, applied in the client on both the streamed and non-streamed paths and again at dispatch. Running it twice is harmless.
  - I checked every property of the four harness tools. Each optional field set to null is stripped: read_file's `start_line`, `end_line`, `offset` and `length`, write_file's `overwrite`, edit_file's `replace_all`, and run_command's `cwd`, `timeout_secs`, `shell`, `background` and `service_name`.
  - Required fields set to null are kept, so they are still rejected.
  - `false`, `0` and `""` are not stripped.
  - For a well-formed native call through the agent, the observation key is `{"path":"a.txt"}` and read coverage is recorded.
- Not fixed. Calls that bypass the client normaliser still reach `observationKey` and `readRequests` with nulls in `call.function.arguments`. That covers arguments that need JSON repair (unclosed or trailing-brace) and text-mode tool calls. I saw the effect with an instrumented probe: read coverage was `NOCOVERAGE` for repairable null arguments and `COVERAGE` for clean ones, and the keys differed. The dispatch-time strip only changes the local `args`, not the call object. This is narrow, because vLLM strict normally emits valid JSON. It is still a real inconsistency.
- Over-broad. The normaliser strips a null from every optional property of every tool, for all providers. A property whose schema explicitly allows null (for example an MCP tool taking `due: ["string","null"]`) loses its meaningful null. I measured this on a synthetic tool and did not check which real MCP tools are affected.

**C. Transient `/version` failure on a successful rediscovery: PARTIAL.**
- At 0d506762 the vLLM kind and strict mode are retained. At b032791e the kind became `unknown`.
- The returned advertisement still has `via:"catalog"` instead of `"vllm"`.
- In `agent.ts` the request-counting gate (`measureAdmission`) allows tokenizing only for `via` of `llamacpp` or `vllm`, or `tokenize:true`. So after such a refresh the agent probably reports "no request counting", even though the client still holds the vLLM kind. I measured the `via` value. I did not run the agent end to end.
- The same gate loses counting after a 404 or missing-model rediscovery, because `refreshCapabilities` drops the advertisement for `unsupported`. The `tokens.test.ts` test calls `countRequestTokens` directly, so it bypasses that gate.

**D. Matrix runner (read-only, tested on scratch copies): FIXED, with a residual.**
- `sourceCommit` is now checked against Git `HEAD` of a caller-named `candidateCheckoutRoot`. That root must be a real directory (not a symlink) and the worktree top level. The checkout must be clean (`--untracked-files=all`, so ignored files do not count).
- `archiveSha256` is now required. It accepts a `sha256:` prefix and uppercase, and rejects empty, missing or wrong values.
- My attempts:
  - A symlinked `installRoot`, a symlinked checkout root and a symlinked `lib/` inside the install were all rejected.
  - A subdirectory of the checkout was rejected.
  - A post-verify swap of `installRoot` does not matter, because the runtime is imported from the private verified archive snapshot.
- Residual, LOW-MEDIUM. The checkout is not bound to the archive contents. A handwritten dist with a matching manifest, forged provenance, and any unrelated clean repo whose `HEAD` equals the claimed commit passes verification and reaches the provider stage. The author's own fixture does exactly this. The commit proves only that the commit hash exists in a clean checkout.

**E. Test quality: PARTIAL.**
- Mutation results, with the focused test files run per mutation:

|---|---|

| `/version` retention reverted | `client_tool_grammar` fails |

- The new `agent_observer` test does not isolate what it names. It passes with either normaliser removed alone, because it checks the observer's post-dispatch `args`, not `call.function.arguments`.
- The "required null is rejected" assertion in `vllm_nullable_dispatch` also passes if required nulls are stripped, because the error text matches either way.
- Retention on 404, bad JSON and a dropped connection has no test. The vLLM retention tests are covered only for 503 (tokens) and `/version` failure.

- A reminder about the mutation runs: they used scratch copies that symlink `docs` and `core`, so `ACC-INF-08` failed in every one of them. That is an artefact of my setup, not a defect. It passed in the real worktree.
